1. Notes Example / You Try It

Section 5.1 - MVT, EVT, and Critical Points

U5-S1-NOTE-EX01
Section 5.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

For $f(x)=x^{2} + 2 x + 1$ on $[-1,3]$, verify the Mean Value Theorem hypotheses and find the value $c$ guaranteed by the theorem.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $c=1$
Solution / scoring notes: The polynomial is continuous on the closed interval and differentiable on the open interval. The secant slope is $4$; solve $f'(c)=4$.
U5-S1-NOTE-YTI01
Section 5.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Does the Mean Value Theorem apply to $f(x)=|x|$ on $[-2,2]$? Justify using the theorem hypotheses.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: No.
Solution / scoring notes: The function is continuous, but it is not differentiable at 0 inside the interval.
U5-S1-NOTE-EX02
Section 5.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

U5-S1-NOTE-YTI02
Section 5.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 1

You Try It

A differentiable function has $f'(x)=x^{2} + 3 x + 2$. Identify its critical numbers.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $x=-2, -1$
Solution / scoring notes: Critical numbers occur where the derivative is zero or undefined; here solve the factored polynomial equation.
U5-S1-NOTE-EX03
Section 5.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 1

Example

A differentiable function has $f'(x)=x^{2} - 5 x + 4$. Identify its critical numbers.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $x=1, 4$
Solution / scoring notes: Critical numbers occur where the derivative is zero or undefined; here solve the factored polynomial equation.
U5-S1-NOTE-YTI03
Section 5.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Find the absolute maximum and minimum values of $f(x)=x^{3} + 3 x^{2} - 2$ on $[-3,1]$. Show how critical points and endpoints enter the comparison.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: Absolute maximum $2$ at $x=-2$; absolute minimum $-2$ at $x=-3$.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate, find interior critical numbers, evaluate the function at every critical number and endpoint, then compare values.
U5-S1-NOTE-EX04
Section 5.1 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Find the absolute maximum and minimum values of $f(x)=x^{3} + 3 x^{2} - 4$ on $[-3,1]$. Show how critical points and endpoints enter the comparison.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Absolute maximum $0$ at $x=-2$; absolute minimum $-4$ at $x=-3$.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate, find interior critical numbers, evaluate the function at every critical number and endpoint, then compare values.
U5-S1-NOTE-YTI04
Section 5.1 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 3

You Try It

A student says, "Every point where $f'(x)=0$ is a local extremum." Evaluate the claim and provide a counterexample or missing condition.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: The claim is false.
Solution / scoring notes: A critical point is only a candidate. For example, $f(x)=x^3$ has $f'(0)=0$ but no local extremum at 0.

Section 5.2 - Increasing, Decreasing, and First Derivative Test

U5-S2-NOTE-EX01
Section 5.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Use the sign chart to state where $f$ is increasing and decreasing.
$x$$x\lt -2$$-2\lt x\lt 2$$x\gt 2$
$f'$-+-

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Increasing: $(-2,2)$; decreasing: $(-\infty,-2)$, $(2,\infty)$.
Solution / scoring notes: A function increases where its first derivative is positive and decreases where the first derivative is negative.
U5-S2-NOTE-YTI01
Section 5.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Given $f'(x)=- x^{2} + 3 x + 4$, classify each critical number with the first derivative test.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: Local minimum at $x=-1$; local maximum at $x=4$.
Solution / scoring notes: Factor or use the given factors, make a sign chart for the derivative, and classify by sign changes.
U5-S2-NOTE-EX02
Section 5.2 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Given $f'(x)=x^{2} - 16$, classify each critical number with the first derivative test.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Local maximum at $x=-4$; local minimum at $x=4$.
Solution / scoring notes: Factor or use the given factors, make a sign chart for the derivative, and classify by sign changes.
U5-S2-NOTE-YTI02
Section 5.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

For $f'(x)=- x^{2} - 2 x + 8$, state the open intervals on which $f$ is increasing.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $(-4,2)$
Solution / scoring notes: Solve where the derivative is positive and report open intervals.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $(-\infty,-1)$ and $(1,\infty)$
Solution / scoring notes: Solve where the derivative is positive and report open intervals.

The figure shows the graph of $f'$. Use its zeros and signs to identify the intervals where $f$ increases and decreases.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: Increasing: $(-3,1)$; decreasing: $(-\infty,-3)$ and $(1,\infty)$.
Solution / scoring notes: Read the sign of the derivative above/below the x-axis.

U5-S2-NOTE-YTI04
Section 5.2 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 3

You Try It

A student argues that $f$ is increasing wherever $f(x)\gt 0$. Diagnose the error and state the correct criterion.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: The sign of the function value does not determine monotonicity; the sign of $f'$ does.
Solution / scoring notes: Use $f'(x)\gt 0$ for increasing and $f'(x)\lt 0$ for decreasing.

Section 5.3 - Concavity and Second Derivative Test

U5-S3-NOTE-EX01
Section 5.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Use the sign chart for $f''$ to state the concavity of $f$.
$x$$x\lt -3$$-3\lt x\lt 4$$x\gt 4$
$f''$-+-

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Concave up: $(-3,4)$; concave down: $(-\infty,-3)$ and $(4,\infty)$.
Solution / scoring notes: Concave up where the second derivative is positive; concave down where it is negative.
U5-S3-NOTE-YTI01
Section 5.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Given $f''(x)=x^{2} + 3 x - 4$, identify the x-values where an inflection point is possible and state the sign-change condition that must be checked.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $x=-4, 1$ are candidates.
Solution / scoring notes: A zero of the second derivative is only a candidate; concavity must change across the point.
U5-S3-NOTE-EX02
Section 5.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Given $f''(x)=- x^{2} + 2 x + 8$, identify the x-values where an inflection point is possible and state the sign-change condition that must be checked.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $x=-2, 4$ are candidates.
Solution / scoring notes: A zero of the second derivative is only a candidate; concavity must change across the point.
U5-S3-NOTE-YTI02
Section 5.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

At $x=3$, suppose $f'(x)=0$ and $f''(3)=3$. Classify the critical point with the second derivative test.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: local minimum at $x=3$.
Solution / scoring notes: A positive second derivative at a critical point gives a local minimum; a negative second derivative gives a local maximum.
U5-S3-NOTE-EX03
Section 5.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

At $x=-2$, suppose $f'(x)=0$ and $f''(-2)=5$. Classify the critical point with the second derivative test.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: local minimum at $x=-2$.
Solution / scoring notes: A positive second derivative at a critical point gives a local minimum; a negative second derivative gives a local maximum.
U5-S3-NOTE-YTI03
Section 5.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

The graph shown is $f''$. Determine the intervals where $f$ is concave up and concave down.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: Concave up: $(-1.001,3.001)$; concave down: $(-\infty,-1.001)$ and $(3.001,\infty)$.
Solution / scoring notes: Use the sign of the second derivative.
U5-S3-NOTE-EX04
Section 5.3 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

The graph shown is $f''$. Determine the intervals where $f$ is concave up and concave down.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Concave up: $(0.996,4.004)$; concave down: $(-\infty,0.996)$ and $(4.004,\infty)$.
Solution / scoring notes: Use the sign of the second derivative.
U5-S3-NOTE-YTI04
Section 5.3 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 3

You Try It

A student claims that $f''(2)=0$ proves $x=2$ is an inflection point. Explain what additional evidence is required.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: One must show that concavity changes at $x=2$.
Solution / scoring notes: A zero or undefined second derivative is only a candidate location; the sign of the second derivative must change.

Section 5.4 - Connecting f, f', and f''

U5-S4-NOTE-EX01
Section 5.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Use the table to identify which listed x-values are critical-point candidates for $f$ and which listed x-value is an inflection-point candidate.
$x$$f'(x)$$f''(x)$
$-2$$3$$-1$
$-1$$0$$-2$
$0$$-2$$0$
$1$$0$$3$
$2$$4$$5$

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Critical-point candidates: $x=-1,1$; inflection candidate: $x=0$.
Solution / scoring notes: Critical candidates occur where the first derivative is zero; inflection candidates occur where the second derivative is zero or undefined and require a concavity change check.
U5-S4-NOTE-YTI01
Section 5.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 3

You Try It

The figure shows $f'$. Describe how its zeros and increasing/decreasing portions determine local extrema and concavity information for $f$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: Zeros with sign change classify extrema of $f$; where $f'$ is increasing, $f$ is concave up, and where $f'$ is decreasing, $f$ is concave down.
Solution / scoring notes: Use both the sign and the trend of the first derivative rather than matching graph shapes.
U5-S4-NOTE-EX02
Section 5.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 3

Example

The figure shows $f'$. Describe how its zeros and increasing/decreasing portions determine local extrema and concavity information for $f$.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

U5-S4-NOTE-YTI02
Section 5.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

If $f'(c)=0$ and $f''(c)\gt 0$, state what each derivative fact says and give the combined conclusion about $f$ at $x=c$.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: The first derivative makes $c$ critical; the positive second derivative shows concave up, so $f$ has a local minimum at $c$.
Solution / scoring notes: Combine first- and second-derivative evidence.
U5-S4-NOTE-EX03
Section 5.4 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

U5-S4-NOTE-YTI03
Section 5.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 3

You Try It

The figure displays $f$, $f'$, and $f''$ together. Choose one local extremum of $f$ and explain how both derivative graphs support the classification.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: A valid response identifies a zero/sign change of $f'$ and consistent concavity from $f''$ at the same x-value.
Solution / scoring notes: The explanation must coordinate the labeled representations, not rely on visual shape alone.

U5-S4-NOTE-YTI04
Section 5.4 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 3

You Try It

A student matches a graph of $f$ to a graph of $f'$ only because the curves look similar. Give two derivative relationships that should be checked instead.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: Check that zeros of $f'$ align with horizontal tangents/critical points of $f$, and that the sign of $f'$ agrees with intervals where $f$ increases or decreases.
Solution / scoring notes: Derivative graphs are connected by rate information, not by shape resemblance.

Section 5.5 - Optimization

U5-S5-NOTE-EX01
Section 5.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A rectangle has perimeter 64. Let one side be $x$. Write a one-variable area function with its feasible domain, then determine the dimensions that maximize area.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Maximum-area dimensions are $16$ by $16$.
Solution / scoring notes: The constraint gives the other side $32-x$; maximize $A(x)=x(32-x)$ on $0\lt x\lt 32$.
U5-S5-NOTE-YTI01
Section 5.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

A farmer has 72 meters of fence for three sides of a rectangle along a straight river. Let $x$ be each side perpendicular to the river. Construct the area objective and find the dimensions that maximize area.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: Perpendicular sides $18$ m; river-parallel side $36$ m.
Solution / scoring notes: Constraint: $2x+y=72$; objective $A(x)=x(72-2x)$. Set $A'(x)=0$ and check the feasible domain.
U5-S5-NOTE-EX02
Section 5.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

A farmer has 96 meters of fence for three sides of a rectangle along a straight river. Let $x$ be each side perpendicular to the river. Construct the area objective and find the dimensions that maximize area.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: Perpendicular sides $24$ m; river-parallel side $48$ m.
Solution / scoring notes: Constraint: $2x+y=96$; objective $A(x)=x(96-2x)$. Set $A'(x)=0$ and check the feasible domain.
U5-S5-NOTE-YTI02
Section 5.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Squares of side $x$ are cut from each corner of a 30-cm square sheet and the sides are folded to make an open box. Write the volume objective and find the interior cut size that gives the maximum volume.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $x=5$ cm
Solution / scoring notes: Use $V(x)=x(30-2x)^2$ on $0\lt x\lt 15$. The relevant interior critical point is $x=5$.
U5-S5-NOTE-EX03
Section 5.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Example

Squares of side $x$ are cut from each corner of a 18-cm square sheet and the sides are folded to make an open box. Write the volume objective and find the interior cut size that gives the maximum volume.

Organize the work so the rule, definition, or substitution can be followed line by line.

Answer: $x=3$ cm
Solution / scoring notes: Use $V(x)=x(18-2x)^2$ on $0\lt x\lt 9$. The relevant interior critical point is $x=3$.
U5-S5-NOTE-YTI03
Section 5.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 2

You Try It

Two positive numbers have sum 20. Model their product as a one-variable function and determine the maximum product.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: $100$
Solution / scoring notes: Let the numbers be $x$ and $20-x$. Then $P(x)=x(20-x)$ has its maximum at $x=10$.
U5-S5-NOTE-EX04
Section 5.5 | Notes Example | Current | DOK 2

Two positive numbers have sum 20. Model their product as a one-variable function and determine the maximum product.

U5-S5-NOTE-YTI04
Section 5.5 | Notes You Try It | Current | DOK 3

You Try It

A solution to an optimization problem finds one critical value and stops. Explain why this is not yet a complete justification for an absolute optimum on a feasible interval.

Record the final result in a complete mathematical statement.

Answer: The critical value must be compared with all feasible candidates, including endpoints when they belong to the domain, and the objective function/domain must be the correct model.
Solution / scoring notes: Optimization requires both model validity and extremum justification.

Answer: No.
Solution / scoring notes: The function is continuous, but it is not differentiable at 0 inside the interval.

U5-S1-PS1-C03

Find the absolute maximum and minimum values of $f(x)=x^{2} \left(x - 3\right)$ on $[-1,3]$. Show how critical points and endpoints enter the comparison.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Absolute maximum $0$ at $x=0$; absolute minimum $-4$ at $x=-1$.

U5-S1-PS1-C04
Section 5.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 3
A student says, "Every point where $f'(x)=0$ is a local extremum." Evaluate the claim and provide a counterexample or missing condition.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: The claim is false.
Solution / scoring notes: A critical point is only a candidate. For example, $f(x)=x^3$ has $f'(0)=0$ but no local extremum at 0.
U5-S1-PS1-C05
Section 5.1 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
State the conclusion of the Mean Value Theorem in slope language after all hypotheses have been verified on $[a,b]$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $f'(c)=\frac{f(b)-f(a)}{b-a}$ for some $c\in(a,b)$
Solution / scoring notes: The theorem equates an instantaneous tangent slope to the average secant slope at some interior point.
U5-S1-PS1-L01
Section 5.1 | Practice Set 1 | Last | DOK 3
A student sees a quotient limit with finite nonzero numerator and zero denominator and immediately applies $L'Hospital$'s Rule. Explain the eligibility error.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: The quotient is not automatically in the required $0/0$ or $\infty/\infty$ indeterminate form.
Solution / scoring notes: Check the original limit form before differentiating numerator and denominator.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $4.88$
Solution / scoring notes: Evaluate the tangent-line model at the nearby input.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $4$
Solution / scoring notes: Direct substitution gives $0/0$; differentiating numerator and denominator gives the limit $4$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $\frac{23}{5}$

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $0/0$ or $\infty/\infty$
Solution / scoring notes: The theorem applies to quotient limits that first produce one of these indeterminate forms and satisfy its hypotheses.
U5-S1-PS1-M03
Section 5.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
A particle has position $s(t)=t^3-3t^2+2t$. Determine the signs of velocity and acceleration at $t=2$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $v=2$, $a=6$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate twice: $v(t)=3 x^{2} - 6 x + 2$ and $a(t)=6 x - 6$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $8$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor the numerator, cancel on the punctured neighborhood, and substitute into the simplified expression.
U5-S1-PS1-M05
Section 5.1 | Practice Set 1 | Mastery | DOK 2
A differentiable function satisfies $F(2)=7$ and $F'(2)=-3$. Interpret $F'(2)=-3$ as a local rate statement.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Near the input 2, the output is decreasing at about 3 output-units per input-unit.
Solution / scoring notes: A derivative is an instantaneous rate of change at the stated input.
U5-S1-PS1-M06

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $9 x^{2} - 4 x + 3$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the product rule, then simplify.
U5-S1-PS1-M07

Differentiate $g(x)=\left(2 x - 3\right)^{3}$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $g'(x)=6 \left(2 x - 3\right)^{2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Apply the chain rule and simplify.

A quantity $Q(t)$ is measured in degrees Celsius and time in hours. State the units of $Q'(t)$.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: degrees Celsius per hours
Solution / scoring notes: A derivative has output units divided by input units.

Section 5.2 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2

Solution / scoring notes: Combine first- and second-derivative evidence.

Section 5.5 - Optimization

U5-S5-PS1-C01
Section 5.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
A farmer has 72 meters of fence for three sides of a rectangle along a straight river. Let $x$ be each side perpendicular to the river. Construct the area objective and find the dimensions that maximize area.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: Perpendicular sides $18$ m; river-parallel side $36$ m.
Solution / scoring notes: Constraint: $2x+y=72$; objective $A(x)=x(72-2x)$. Set $A'(x)=0$ and check the feasible domain.
U5-S5-PS1-C02
Section 5.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Squares of side $x$ are cut from each corner of a 18-cm square sheet and the sides are folded to make an open box. Write the volume objective and find the interior cut size that gives the maximum volume.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $x=3$ cm
Solution / scoring notes: Use $V(x)=x(18-2x)^2$ on $0\lt x\lt 9$. The relevant interior critical point is $x=3$.
U5-S5-PS1-C03
Section 5.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 2
Two positive numbers have sum 20. Model their product as a one-variable function and determine the maximum product.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: $100$
Solution / scoring notes: Let the numbers be $x$ and $20-x$. Then $P(x)=x(20-x)$ has its maximum at $x=10$.
U5-S5-PS1-C04
Section 5.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 3
A solution to an optimization problem finds one critical value and stops. Explain why this is not yet a complete justification for an absolute optimum on a feasible interval.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: The critical value must be compared with all feasible candidates, including endpoints when they belong to the domain, and the objective function/domain must be the correct model.
Solution / scoring notes: Optimization requires both model validity and extremum justification.
U5-S5-PS1-C05
Section 5.5 | Practice Set 1 | Current | DOK 1
In an optimization model, distinguish the constraint from the objective function in one sentence.

Show the setup before you simplify or evaluate.

Answer: The constraint restricts allowable quantities; the objective is the quantity being maximized or minimized.
Solution / scoring notes: The two equations play different roles and should not be interchanged.

U5-S1-PS2-C03

Answer: The claim is false.

U5-S1-PS2-C04
Section 5.1 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
State the conclusion of the Mean Value Theorem in slope language after all hypotheses have been verified on $[a,b]$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $f'(c)=\frac{f(b)-f(a)}{b-a}$ for some $c\in(a,b)$
Solution / scoring notes: The theorem equates an instantaneous tangent slope to the average secant slope at some interior point.

Answer: $c=-2$
Solution / scoring notes: The polynomial is continuous on the closed interval and differentiable on the open interval. The secant slope is $-1$; solve $f'(c)=-1$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Use a complete sentence when the response is an interpretation. Focus the response on the units interpretation requested here.

Answer: degrees Celsius per hours
Solution / scoring notes: A derivative has output units divided by input units.
U5-S3-PS2-M02
Section 5.3 | Practice Set 2 | Mastery | DOK 2
Using the linearization data $f(3)=4$ and $f'(3)=3$, estimate $f(\frac{16}{5})$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $\frac{23}{5}$
Solution / scoring notes: Use $L(x)=f(a)+f'(a)(x-a)$; the estimate is $\frac{23}{5}$.
U5-S3-PS2-M03
Section 5.3 | Practice Set 2 | Mastery | DOK 1
Which indeterminate form is required before applying $L'Hospital$'s Rule to a quotient limit?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Write only the information needed to resolve the stated question. Focus the response on the lhospital condition retrieval requested here.

Answer: $0/0$ or $\infty/\infty$
Solution / scoring notes: The theorem applies to quotient limits that first produce one of these indeterminate forms and satisfy its hypotheses.
U5-S3-PS2-M04
Section 5.3 | Practice Set 2 | Mastery | DOK 2
A particle has position $s(t)=t^3-3t^2+2t$. Determine the signs of velocity and acceleration at $t=2$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Make the concluding notation unambiguous about the point being discussed. Focus the response on the motion sign retrieval requested here.

Answer: $v=2$, $a=6$
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiate twice: $v(t)=3 x^{2} - 6 x + 2$ and $a(t)=6 x - 6$.
U5-S3-PS2-M05

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $6$
Solution / scoring notes: Factor the numerator, cancel on the punctured neighborhood, and substitute into the simplified expression.
U5-S3-PS2-M06

Answer: Near the input 2, the output is decreasing at about 3 output-units per input-unit.
Solution / scoring notes: A derivative is an instantaneous rate of change at the stated input.

Answer: $9 x^{2} - 4 x + 3$

Section 5.4 - Connecting f, f', and f''

U5-S4-PS2-C01
Section 5.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
If $f'(c)=0$ and $f''(c)\gt 0$, state what each derivative fact says and give the combined conclusion about $f$ at $x=c$.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: The first derivative makes $c$ critical; the positive second derivative shows concave up, so $f$ has a local minimum at $c$.
Solution / scoring notes: Combine first- and second-derivative evidence.
U5-S4-PS2-C02
Section 5.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
The figure displays $f$, $f'$, and $f''$ together. Choose one local extremum of $f$ and explain how both derivative graphs support the classification.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: A valid response identifies a zero/sign change of $f'$ and consistent concavity from $f''$ at the same x-value.
Solution / scoring notes: The explanation must coordinate the labeled representations, not rely on visual shape alone.
U5-S4-PS2-C03
Section 5.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
A student matches a graph of $f$ to a graph of $f'$ only because the curves look similar. Give two derivative relationships that should be checked instead.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Check that zeros of $f'$ align with horizontal tangents/critical points of $f$, and that the sign of $f'$ agrees with intervals where $f$ increases or decreases.
Solution / scoring notes: Derivative graphs are connected by rate information, not by shape resemblance.
U5-S4-PS2-C04
Section 5.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
If $f'$ has a local maximum at $x=a$ and is differentiable there, what is a likely value of $f''(a)$?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $0$
Solution / scoring notes: At a differentiable local extremum of the first derivative, its derivative, which is the second derivative of the original function, is zero.
U5-S4-PS2-C05
Section 5.4 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Use the table to identify which listed x-values are critical-point candidates for $f$ and which listed x-value is an inflection-point candidate.
$x$$f'(x)$$f''(x)$
$-2$$3$$-1$
$-1$$0$$-2$
$0$$-2$$0$
$1$$0$$3$
$2$$4$$5$

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Critical-point candidates: $x=-1,1$; inflection candidate: $x=0$.
Solution / scoring notes: Critical candidates occur where the first derivative is zero; inflection candidates occur where the second derivative is zero or undefined and require a concavity change check.
U5-S4-PS2-L01
Section 5.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 1
A function is concave up on an interval. Which statement about $f'$ is necessarily true there?

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Place the requested quantity first in the final response. Focus the response on the concavity recognition requested here.

Answer: $f'$ is increasing on the interval.
Solution / scoring notes: Concavity describes how the first derivative changes, not whether the original function is positive or increasing.
U5-S4-PS2-L02
Section 5.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
Use the sign chart for $f''$ to state the concavity of $f$.
$x$$x\lt 1$$1\lt x\lt 2$$x\gt 2$
$f''$-+-

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Concave up: $(1,2)$; concave down: $(-\infty,1)$ and $(2,\infty)$.
Solution / scoring notes: Concave up where the second derivative is positive; concave down where it is negative.
U5-S4-PS2-L03
Section 5.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
Given $f''(x)=x^{2} - 6 x + 8$, identify the x-values where an inflection point is possible and state the sign-change condition that must be checked.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $x=2, 4$ are candidates.
Solution / scoring notes: A zero of the second derivative is only a candidate; concavity must change across the point.
U5-S4-PS2-L04
Section 5.4 | Practice Set 2 | Last | DOK 2
At $x=3$, suppose $f'(x)=0$ and $f''(3)=5$. Classify the critical point with the second derivative test.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: local minimum at $x=3$.
Solution / scoring notes: A positive second derivative at a critical point gives a local minimum; a negative second derivative gives a local maximum.

Answer: Concave up: $(-\infty,-2.99)$ and $(-1.01,\infty)$; concave down: $(-2.99,-1.01)$.
Solution / scoring notes: Use the sign of the second derivative.

Section 5.5 - Optimization

U5-S5-PS2-C01
Section 5.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Squares of side $x$ are cut from each corner of a 30-cm square sheet and the sides are folded to make an open box. Write the volume objective and find the interior cut size that gives the maximum volume.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $x=5$ cm
Solution / scoring notes: Use $V(x)=x(30-2x)^2$ on $0\lt x\lt 15$. The relevant interior critical point is $x=5$.
U5-S5-PS2-C02
Section 5.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
Two positive numbers have sum 30. Model their product as a one-variable function and determine the maximum product.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: $225$
Solution / scoring notes: Let the numbers be $x$ and $30-x$. Then $P(x)=x(30-x)$ has its maximum at $x=15$.
U5-S5-PS2-C03
Section 5.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 3
A solution to an optimization problem finds one critical value and stops. Explain why this is not yet a complete justification for an absolute optimum on a feasible interval.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: The critical value must be compared with all feasible candidates, including endpoints when they belong to the domain, and the objective function/domain must be the correct model.
Solution / scoring notes: Optimization requires both model validity and extremum justification.
U5-S5-PS2-C04
Section 5.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 1
In an optimization model, distinguish the constraint from the objective function in one sentence.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: The constraint restricts allowable quantities; the objective is the quantity being maximized or minimized.
Solution / scoring notes: The two equations play different roles and should not be interchanged.
U5-S5-PS2-C05
Section 5.5 | Practice Set 2 | Current | DOK 2
A rectangle has perimeter 56. Let one side be $x$. Write a one-variable area function with its feasible domain, then determine the dimensions that maximize area.

Keep the key rule or definition visible in your work.

Answer: Maximum-area dimensions are $14$ by $14$.
Solution / scoring notes: The constraint gives the other side $28-x$; maximize $A(x)=x(28-x)$ on $0\lt x\lt 28$.

Section 5.1 - MVT, EVT, and Critical Points

U5-S1-XP-C01
Section 5.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
A student says, "Every point where $f'(x)=0$ is a local extremum." Evaluate the claim and provide a counterexample or missing condition.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The claim is false.
Solution / scoring notes: A critical point is only a candidate. For example, $f(x)=x^3$ has $f'(0)=0$ but no local extremum at 0.
U5-S1-XP-C02
Section 5.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 1
State the conclusion of the Mean Value Theorem in slope language after all hypotheses have been verified on $[a,b]$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $f'(c)=\frac{f(b)-f(a)}{b-a}$ for some $c\in(a,b)$
Solution / scoring notes: The theorem equates an instantaneous tangent slope to the average secant slope at some interior point.
U5-S1-XP-C03
Section 5.1 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
For $f(x)=x \left(x + 2\right)$ on $[-2,2]$, verify the Mean Value Theorem hypotheses and find the value $c$ guaranteed by the theorem.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $c=0$
Solution / scoring notes: The polynomial is continuous on the closed interval and differentiable on the open interval. The secant slope is $2$; solve $f'(c)=2$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Yes.
Solution / scoring notes: A polynomial is continuous on the closed bounded interval, so EVT guarantees both absolute extrema.

Section 5.4 - Connecting f, f', and f''

U5-S4-XP-C01
Section 5.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 3
A student matches a graph of $f$ to a graph of $f'$ only because the curves look similar. Give two derivative relationships that should be checked instead.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Check that zeros of $f'$ align with horizontal tangents/critical points of $f$, and that the sign of $f'$ agrees with intervals where $f$ increases or decreases.
Solution / scoring notes: Derivative graphs are connected by rate information, not by shape resemblance.
U5-S4-XP-C02
Section 5.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2
If $f'$ has a local maximum at $x=a$ and is differentiable there, what is a likely value of $f''(a)$?

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: $0$
Solution / scoring notes: At a differentiable local extremum of the first derivative, its derivative, which is the second derivative of the original function, is zero.
U5-S4-XP-C03
Section 5.4 | Extra Practice | Current | DOK 2

$x$$f'(x)$$f''(x)$
$-2$$3$$-1$
$-1$$0$$-2$
$0$$-2$$0$
$1$$0$$3$
$2$$4$$5$

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Critical-point candidates: $x=-1,1$; inflection candidate: $x=0$.
Solution / scoring notes: Critical candidates occur where the first derivative is zero; inflection candidates occur where the second derivative is zero or undefined and require a concavity change check.

Answer: Zeros with sign change classify extrema of $f$; where $f'$ is increasing, $f$ is concave up, and where $f'$ is decreasing, $f$ is concave down.
Solution / scoring notes: Use both the sign and the trend of the first derivative rather than matching graph shapes.

Solution / scoring notes: Combine first- and second-derivative evidence.

Section 5.5 - Optimization

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The critical value must be compared with all feasible candidates, including endpoints when they belong to the domain, and the objective function/domain must be the correct model.
Solution / scoring notes: Optimization requires both model validity and extremum justification.
U5-S5-XP-C02

In an optimization model, distinguish the constraint from the objective function in one sentence.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: The constraint restricts allowable quantities; the objective is the quantity being maximized or minimized.
Solution / scoring notes: The two equations play different roles and should not be interchanged.

Solution / scoring notes: The constraint gives the other side $20-x$; maximize $A(x)=x(20-x)$ on $0\lt x\lt 20$.

Write the final quantity with notation that matches the prompt.

Answer: Perpendicular sides $24$ m; river-parallel side $48$ m.
Solution / scoring notes: Constraint: $2x+y=96$; objective $A(x)=x(96-2x)$. Set $A'(x)=0$ and check the feasible domain.

Does the Extreme Value Theorem guarantee an absolute maximum and minimum for $f(x)=x^3-2x+1$ on $[-2,3]$? State the hypotheses that support your answer.

Clearly mark the quantity that answers the question.

Answer: Yes.
Solution / scoring notes: A polynomial is continuous on the closed bounded interval, so EVT guarantees both absolute extrema.

Answer: $4$
Solution / scoring notes: Direct substitution gives $0/0$; differentiating numerator and denominator gives the limit $4$.

State the conclusion of the Mean Value Theorem in slope language after all hypotheses have been verified on $[a,b]$.

Use a direct method and keep the response concise.

Answer: $f'(c)=\frac{f(b)-f(a)}{b-a}$ for some $c\in(a,b)$
Solution / scoring notes: The theorem equates an instantaneous tangent slope to the average secant slope at some interior point.

A student argues that $f$ is increasing wherever $f(x)\gt 0$. Diagnose the error and state the correct criterion.

Give the answer and one supporting mathematical step.

Answer: The sign of the function value does not determine monotonicity; the sign of $f'$ does.
Solution / scoring notes: Use $f'(x)\gt 0$ for increasing and $f'(x)\lt 0$ for decreasing.

Answer: A local maximum occurs at $x=3$.
Solution / scoring notes: Positive-to-negative derivative sign change means the function changes from increasing to decreasing.

Solution / scoring notes: The two equations play different roles and should not be interchanged.

9. Performance / Future Tasks

Section 5.1 - MVT, EVT, and Critical Points

U5-S1-PERF01
Section 5.1 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Performance / Future Task

Analyze the three cases below. For each, decide whether the named theorem is eligible, cite every needed hypothesis, and state exactly what conclusion is guaranteed. Case A: $f(x)=x^2+1$ on $[0,3]$ for MVT. Case B: $g(x)=|x|$ on $[-2,2]$ for MVT. Case C: $h(x)=1/(x-1)$ on $[0,2]$ for EVT. Then revise one ineligible case by changing only the interval so the theorem becomes eligible.

Support every conclusion with the derivative, theorem, or domain evidence requested.

Expected evidence: The response must distinguish hypotheses from conclusions and provide a valid repaired interval for one failed case.
Scoring criteria: Correct eligibility decisions; all relevant hypotheses; theorem-specific conclusions; valid revision with explanation.
Estimated student time: 20-25 minutes
Answer: Case A is eligible for MVT; B fails differentiability at 0; C fails continuity on the interval. A valid revision removes the problematic point from the interval while preserving a closed interval for EVT or a valid open interior for MVT.
Solution / scoring notes: The response must distinguish hypotheses from conclusions and provide a valid repaired interval for one failed case.

Section 5.2 - Increasing, Decreasing, and First Derivative Test

U5-S2-PERF01
Section 5.2 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 3

Performance / Future Task

A function has critical numbers at $x=-2,1,4$. Its derivative is positive on $(-\infty,-2)$, negative on $(-2,1)$, negative on $(1,4)$, and positive on $(4,\infty)$. Create a first-derivative sign chart, classify every critical number, and audit the claim "all three critical numbers are local extrema." Explain why each classification follows from the signs rather than from the existence of a critical number alone.

Support every conclusion with the derivative, theorem, or domain evidence requested.

Expected evidence: A complete response includes the sign chart, all three classifications, and a repaired claim.
Scoring criteria: Correct sign chart; classifications; explicit sign-change reasoning; critique of the false claim.
Estimated student time: 15-20 minutes
Answer: There is a local maximum at $x=-2$, no local extremum at $x=1$, and a local minimum at $x=4$.
Solution / scoring notes: A complete response includes the sign chart, all three classifications, and a repaired claim.

Performance / Future Task

Two students analyze a critical point $x=2$. Student A knows $f'(2)=0$ and $f''(2)=0$ and concludes there is a local minimum. Student B knows $f'$ changes from negative to positive at 2 and concludes there is a local minimum. Compare the strength of the two arguments, explain why one is conclusive and the other is not, and describe one additional piece of evidence that could make the inconclusive approach decisive.

Support every conclusion with the derivative, theorem, or domain evidence requested.

Expected evidence: The response must compare methods rather than merely state the answer.
Scoring criteria: Correct conclusion; explanation of inconclusive second derivative test; valid additional evidence; comparison of evidence quality.
Estimated student time: 15-20 minutes
Answer: Student B has conclusive first-derivative-test evidence. Student A has an inconclusive second-derivative test because the second derivative is zero.
Solution / scoring notes: The response must compare methods rather than merely state the answer.

Section 5.4 - Connecting f, f', and f''

Performance / Future Task

Construct one possible differentiable function $f$ satisfying all constraints: $f'(x)\gt 0$ for $x\lt -2$ and $x\gt 3$, $f'(x)\lt 0$ for $-2\lt x\lt 3$, $f''(x)\lt 0$ for $x\lt 1$, and $f''(x)\gt 0$ for $x\gt 1$. Mark all local extrema and the inflection point on your sketch. Then explain how every marked feature follows from the derivative conditions. If your first sketch violates a condition, revise it and identify the revision.

Section 5.5 - Optimization

U5-S5-PERF01
Section 5.5 | Performance Future Task | Performance | DOK 4

Performance / Future Task

Design a rectangular display pen against an existing wall using at most 72 meters of new fencing for the other three sides. The enclosed area must be at least 600 square meters. Determine the dimensions that maximize area, decide whether the 600-square-meter requirement is feasible under the optimal design, and write a short defense that identifies the constraint, objective, feasible domain, critical point, and why the final choice is an absolute maximum. Then test how the optimum changes if only 60 meters of fencing are available.

Support every conclusion with the derivative, theorem, or domain evidence requested.

Expected evidence: The work must show a one-variable model and calculus/domain justification for both fencing amounts.
Scoring criteria: Correct constraints and objectives; feasible domains; derivative work; absolute-maximum justification; feasibility conclusion; correct comparison case.
Estimated student time: 25-30 minutes
Answer: With 72 m, the maximum occurs at 18 m perpendicular to the wall and 36 m along the wall, giving 648 square meters, so the requirement is feasible. With 60 m, the corresponding optimum is 15 m by 30 m, giving 450 square meters.
Solution / scoring notes: The work must show a one-variable model and calculus/domain justification for both fencing amounts.

DOK 2 - Theorem Application

U5-FA-D2-01-V1
Section 5.1 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Theorem Application

For $f(x)=x^{2} + 8 x + 4$ on $[-4,0]$, verify the Mean Value Theorem hypotheses and find the value $c$ guaranteed by the theorem.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Answer: $c=-2$
Solution / scoring notes: The polynomial is continuous on the closed interval and differentiable on the open interval. The secant slope is $4$; solve $f'(c)=4$.
U5-FA-D2-01-V2
Section 5.1 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Theorem Application

For $f(x)=x^{2} - 3 x + 1$ on $[-1,3]$, verify the Mean Value Theorem hypotheses and find the value $c$ guaranteed by the theorem.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Answer: $c=1$
Solution / scoring notes: The polynomial is continuous on the closed interval and differentiable on the open interval. The secant slope is $-1$; solve $f'(c)=-1$.

DOK 2 - Second Derivative Analysis

U5-FA-D2-03-V1
Section 5.3 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Second Derivative Analysis

Use the sign chart for $f''$ to state the concavity of $f$.
$x$$x\lt -1$$-1\lt x\lt 3$$x\gt 3$
$f''$-+-

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Answer: Concave up: $(-1,3)$; concave down: $(-\infty,-1)$ and $(3,\infty)$.
Solution / scoring notes: Concave up where the second derivative is positive; concave down where it is negative.

DOK 2 - Optimization Setup and Solve

U5-FA-D2-04-V1
Section 5.5 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Optimization Setup and Solve

A rectangle has perimeter 48. Let one side be $x$. Write a one-variable area function with its feasible domain, then determine the dimensions that maximize area.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Answer: Maximum-area dimensions are $12$ by $12$.
Solution / scoring notes: The constraint gives the other side $24-x$; maximize $A(x)=x(24-x)$ on $0\lt x\lt 24$.
U5-FA-D2-04-V2
Section 5.5 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Optimization Setup and Solve

A farmer has 60 meters of fence for three sides of a rectangle along a straight river. Let $x$ be each side perpendicular to the river. Construct the area objective and find the dimensions that maximize area.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Answer: Perpendicular sides $15$ m; river-parallel side $30$ m.
Solution / scoring notes: Constraint: $2x+y=60$; objective $A(x)=x(60-2x)$. Set $A'(x)=0$ and check the feasible domain.
U5-FA-D2-04-V3
Section 5.5 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Optimization Setup and Solve

Squares of side $x$ are cut from each corner of a 18-cm square sheet and the sides are folded to make an open box. Write the volume objective and find the interior cut size that gives the maximum volume.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Answer: $x=3$ cm
Solution / scoring notes: Use $V(x)=x(18-2x)^2$ on $0\lt x\lt 9$. The relevant interior critical point is $x=3$.
U5-FA-D2-04-V4
Section 5.5 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Optimization Setup and Solve

Two positive numbers have sum 24. Model their product as a one-variable function and determine the maximum product.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Answer: $144$
Solution / scoring notes: Let the numbers be $x$ and $24-x$. Then $P(x)=x(24-x)$ has its maximum at $x=12$.
U5-FA-D2-04-V5
Section 5.5 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Optimization Setup and Solve

A rectangle has perimeter 48. Let one side be $x$. Write a one-variable area function with its feasible domain, then determine the dimensions that maximize area.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Do not replace exact notation with a rounded decimal unless the prompt asks for an estimate. Focus the response on the rectangle optimization requested here.

Answer: Maximum-area dimensions are $12$ by $12$.
Solution / scoring notes: The constraint gives the other side $24-x$; maximize $A(x)=x(24-x)$ on $0\lt x\lt 24$.
U5-FA-D2-04-V6

A farmer has 72 meters of fence for three sides of a rectangle along a straight river. Let $x$ be each side perpendicular to the river. Construct the area objective and find the dimensions that maximize area.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Answer: Perpendicular sides $18$ m; river-parallel side $36$ m.
Solution / scoring notes: Constraint: $2x+y=72$; objective $A(x)=x(72-2x)$. Set $A'(x)=0$ and check the feasible domain.
U5-FA-D2-04-V7
Section 5.5 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Optimization Setup and Solve

Squares of side $x$ are cut from each corner of a 18-cm square sheet and the sides are folded to make an open box. Write the volume objective and find the interior cut size that gives the maximum volume.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Do not replace exact notation with a rounded decimal unless the prompt asks for an estimate. Focus the response on the box optimization requested here.

Answer: $x=3$ cm
Solution / scoring notes: Use $V(x)=x(18-2x)^2$ on $0\lt x\lt 9$. The relevant interior critical point is $x=3$.
U5-FA-D2-04-V8
Section 5.5 | Formative Assessment | N-A | DOK 2

Optimization Setup and Solve

Two positive numbers have sum 36. Model their product as a one-variable function and determine the maximum product.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Answer: $324$
Solution / scoring notes: Let the numbers be $x$ and $36-x$. Then $P(x)=x(36-x)$ has its maximum at $x=18$.

Theorem Claim Critique

Answer: The claim is valid because polynomials are continuous; the continuity hypothesis should still be stated explicitly.

DOK 4 - Function Behavior Investigation

U5-FA-D4-01-V1
Section 5.4 | Formative Assessment | Performance | DOK 4

Function Behavior Investigation

Investigate a differentiable function with $f'(-3)=f'(-1)=f'(1)=0$. Its derivative is positive on $(-\infty,-3)$, negative on $(-3,-1)$, positive on $(-1,1)$, and positive on $(1,\infty)$. Its second derivative is negative for $x\lt -1$ and positive for $x\gt -1$. Create a behavior summary that includes monotonicity, all local extrema, concavity, and any inflection point justified by the data. Then test the claim that every derivative zero must be an extremum and revise the claim if necessary.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Answer: There is a local maximum at $x=-3$, a local minimum at $x=-1$, and no extremum forced at $x=1$ because the first derivative stays positive across it. Concavity changes at $x=-1$.
Solution / scoring notes: Full credit requires a coherent behavior map, justified extrema/inflection conclusions, and a repaired general claim.
U5-FA-D4-01-V2

DOK 4 - Optimization Design Task

U5-FA-D4-03-V1
Section 5.5 | Formative Assessment | Performance | DOK 4

Optimization Design Task

Design task: a rectangular enclosure uses 48 meters of fencing for three sides along a wall. Build the constraint and area objective, determine the feasible domain, find and justify the absolute maximum area, then impose an added requirement that the wall-parallel side be at least 16 meters and decide whether the original optimum remains feasible. If not, revise the design.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Answer: Unrestricted optimum: $12$ by $24$, area $288$. The added requirement is satisfied, so the unrestricted optimum remains valid.
Solution / scoring notes: The design must show the one-variable model, derivative evidence, endpoint/domain reasoning, and a constraint-feasibility revision if required.
U5-FA-D4-03-V2
Section 5.5 | Formative Assessment | Performance | DOK 4

Optimization Design Task

Design task: a rectangular enclosure uses 60 meters of fencing for three sides along a wall. Build the constraint and area objective, determine the feasible domain, find and justify the absolute maximum area, then impose an added requirement that the wall-parallel side be at least 36 meters and decide whether the original optimum remains feasible. If not, revise the design.

Graph Reconstruction Performance

Reconstruction task: sketch one differentiable $f$ such that $f'$ is positive before $x=-2$, negative between $x=-2$ and $x=1$, and positive after $x=1$; additionally $f''$ changes from negative to positive at $x=3$. Label the extrema and inflection point, then write a derivative-based verification for every label. Revise the sketch if any condition conflicts with your first attempt.

Respond only from the evidence supplied in this assessment task and justify where requested.

Answer: A valid sketch has a local maximum at $x=-2$, a local minimum at $x=1$, and concavity changing at $x=3$.
Solution / scoring notes: The task is scored on satisfying all derivative constraints, labeled features, and explicit verification/revision.

11. Summative Assessment Source Content

Summative Version 1

U5-SUM-V1-Q01
Section 5.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

Which statement supplies all hypotheses needed for the Mean Value Theorem on $[a,b]$?
  1. Continuous on $[a,b]$ and differentiable on $(a,b)$
  2. Bounded on $[a,b]$
  3. Differentiable only at the endpoints
  4. Continuous only on $[a,b]$

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: A. Continuous on $[a,b]$ and differentiable on $(a,b)$
Solution / scoring notes: MVT requires continuity on the closed interval and differentiability on the open interval.
U5-SUM-V1-Q02
Section 5.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

For $f(x)=x^{2} + 2 x + 4$ on $[-4,2]$, find the value $c$ guaranteed by MVT after verifying its hypotheses.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: $-1$
Solution / scoring notes: The polynomial meets both hypotheses. The secant slope is $0$; solve $f'(c)=0$.
U5-SUM-V1-Q03
Section 5.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

If $f'(x)=x^{2} + 5 x + 6$, which set lists all critical numbers?
  1. ${-2}$
  2. ${-3,-2}$
  3. $\varnothing$
  4. ${-3}$

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: B. ${-3,-2}$
Solution / scoring notes: Critical numbers occur where the derivative is zero or undefined.
U5-SUM-V1-Q04
Section 5.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

A student says EVT guarantees absolute extrema for every differentiable function on any open interval. Critique the claim and state the correct theorem conditions.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: The claim is false. EVT requires continuity on a closed, bounded interval.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiability is stronger than continuity locally but does not replace the closed-interval condition.
U5-SUM-V1-Q05
Section 5.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

For $f'(x)=- x^{2} - 5 x - 6$, on which interval(s) is $f$ increasing?
  1. $(-\infty,-2)$
  2. $(-\infty,-3)$ and $(-2,\infty)$
  3. $(-3,-2)$
  4. $(-3,\infty)$

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: C. $(-3,-2)$
Solution / scoring notes: A function increases where its first derivative is positive.
U5-SUM-V1-Q06
Section 5.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

Use the first derivative test for $f'(x)=- x^{2} + x + 2$ to classify the critical numbers.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: Local minimum at $x=-1$; local maximum at $x=2$.
Solution / scoring notes: Make a sign chart for the factored derivative and classify each sign change.
U5-SUM-V1-Q07
Section 5.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

The figure is the graph of $f'$. Which method directly determines intervals where $f$ increases?
  1. Compare endpoint values of $f$
  2. Find where $f''$ is positive
  3. Read where $f$ is above the x-axis
  4. Read where $f'$ is above the x-axis

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: D. Read where $f'$ is above the x-axis
Solution / scoring notes: Monotonicity of the original function is controlled by the sign of its first derivative.
U5-SUM-V1-Q08
Section 5.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

Two students classify a critical number. One uses only $f'(c)=0$; the other shows $f'$ changes from positive to negative at $c$. Compare the evidence and state which conclusion is justified.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: The sign-change argument justifies a local maximum; derivative zero alone does not classify the point.
Solution / scoring notes: The first derivative test requires behavior on both sides of the critical number.
U5-SUM-V1-Q09
Section 5.3 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

For $f''(x)=- x^{2} + 2 x + 8$, where is $f$ concave up?
  1. $(-2,4)$
  2. $(-\infty,4)$
  3. $(-2,\infty)$
  4. $(-\infty,-2)$ and $(4,\infty)$

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: A. $(-2,4)$
Solution / scoring notes: Concave up corresponds to a positive second derivative.
U5-SUM-V1-Q10
Section 5.3 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

If $f'(-3)=0$ and $f''(-3)=-5$, what does the second derivative test conclude?
  1. local minimum
  2. local maximum
  3. no classification is possible
  4. inflection point

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: B. local maximum
Solution / scoring notes: At a critical point, the sign of the second derivative classifies local curvature when it is nonzero.
U5-SUM-V1-Q11
Section 5.3 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

The figure shows $f''$. State the concavity intervals of $f$ and identify the candidate inflection x-values.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: Concave up: $(-\infty,1.018)$ and $(1.982,\infty)$; concave down: $(1.018,1.982)$; candidates at $1.018$ and $1.982$.
Solution / scoring notes: Use the sign of the second derivative and verify sign changes for inflection points.
U5-SUM-V1-Q12
Section 5.3 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

A critical point has $f'(c)=0$ and $f''(c)=0$. Compare the first- and second-derivative tests as possible next steps and explain which extra evidence could settle the classification.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: The second derivative test is inconclusive; a sign chart for $f'$ around $c$ can classify the point.
Solution / scoring notes: A strategic response recognizes when a test fails and switches to stronger evidence.
U5-SUM-V1-Q13
Section 5.4 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

Which relationship is always correct on an interval where $f''(x)\gt 0$?
  1. $f'$ is positive
  2. $f$ is increasing
  3. $f'$ is increasing
  4. $f$ is positive

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: C. $f'$ is increasing
Solution / scoring notes: Positive second derivative means the first derivative is increasing.
U5-SUM-V1-Q14
Section 5.4 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

At $x=c$, suppose $f'$ changes from negative to positive. Which feature of $f$ is supported?
  1. vertical tangent
  2. inflection point only
  3. local maximum
  4. local minimum

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: D. local minimum
Solution / scoring notes: A negative-to-positive first derivative sign change means decreasing then increasing.
U5-SUM-V1-Q15
Section 5.4 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

Use the table to identify listed critical-point candidates of $f$ and explain what the sign of $f''$ indicates about concavity at the listed nonzero values.
$x$$f'(x)$$f''(x)$
$-2$$2$$-2$
$-1$$0$$-1$
$0$$-3$$0$
$1$$0$$2$
$2$$4$$3$

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: Critical candidates occur at $x=-1,1$; negative $f''$ indicates concave down and positive $f''$ indicates concave up.
Solution / scoring notes: Coordinate the two derivative columns rather than treating them as independent tables.
U5-SUM-V1-Q16
Section 5.4 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

The figure shows $f'$. Develop a derivative-based argument that extracts both extremum information and concavity information for $f$. Explain why sign and trend are different pieces of evidence.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: Zeros/sign changes of $f'$ address extrema; increasing/decreasing behavior of $f'$ addresses concavity.
Solution / scoring notes: The response should use the graph as a derivative representation and distinguish sign from slope/trend.
U5-SUM-V1-Q17
Section 5.5 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

A rectangle has perimeter 48. Which one-variable objective correctly represents its area if one side is $x$?
  1. $A(x)=x(24-x)$
  2. $A(x)=x^2+24$
  3. $A(x)=2x+2(24-x)$
  4. $A(x)=x(48-x)$

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: A. $A(x)=x(24-x)$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the perimeter constraint to express the other side, then multiply side lengths.
U5-SUM-V1-Q18
Section 5.5 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

Two positive numbers have sum 30. Which value of $x$ maximizes $P(x)=x(30-x)$?
  1. $0$
  2. $15$
  3. $\frac{15}{2}$
  4. $30$

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: B. $15$
Solution / scoring notes: The quadratic opens downward; its derivative is zero at half the fixed sum.
U5-SUM-V1-Q19
Section 5.5 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

An open-top box is formed from a 24-cm square by cutting corner squares of side $x$. Write the volume objective, state the feasible domain, and determine the interior maximizing cut size.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: $x=4$ cm
Solution / scoring notes: Use $V(x)=x(24-2x)^2$ on $0\lt x\lt 12$; differentiate and select the feasible interior maximum.
U5-SUM-V1-Q20
Section 5.5 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 5 Summative - Version 1

A proposed optimization solution differentiates a correct objective but never states its feasible domain or compares boundary candidates. Explain why the justification is incomplete and describe the missing checks.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: The feasible domain determines which critical points and endpoints are admissible; all relevant candidates must be checked to justify an absolute optimum.
Solution / scoring notes: A complete defense combines modeling constraints with calculus evidence.

Summative Version 2

U5-SUM-V2-Q01
Section 5.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 5 Summative - Version 2

Which statement supplies all hypotheses needed for the Mean Value Theorem on $[a,b]$?
  1. Bounded on $[a,b]$
  2. Differentiable only at the endpoints
  3. Continuous on $[a,b]$ and differentiable on $(a,b)$
  4. Continuous only on $[a,b]$

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: C. Continuous on $[a,b]$ and differentiable on $(a,b)$
Solution / scoring notes: MVT requires continuity on the closed interval and differentiability on the open interval.
U5-SUM-V2-Q02
Section 5.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 2

For $f(x)=x^{2} + 7 x + 4$ on $[-4,2]$, find the value $c$ guaranteed by MVT after verifying its hypotheses.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: $-1$
Solution / scoring notes: The polynomial meets both hypotheses. The secant slope is $5$; solve $f'(c)=5$.
U5-SUM-V2-Q03
Section 5.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 1

Unit 5 Summative - Version 2

If $f'(x)=x^{2} - 5 x + 6$, which set lists all critical numbers?
  1. ${2}$
  2. $\varnothing$
  3. ${3}$
  4. ${2,3}$

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: D. ${2,3}$
Solution / scoring notes: Critical numbers occur where the derivative is zero or undefined.
U5-SUM-V2-Q04
Section 5.1 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 3

Unit 5 Summative - Version 2

A student says EVT guarantees absolute extrema for every differentiable function on any open interval. Critique the claim and state the correct theorem conditions.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

If a value is an estimate, label it as an estimate rather than an equality. Focus the response on the evt claim critique requested here.

Answer: The claim is false. EVT requires continuity on a closed, bounded interval.
Solution / scoring notes: Differentiability is stronger than continuity locally but does not replace the closed-interval condition.
U5-SUM-V2-Q05
Section 5.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 2

For $f'(x)=x^{2} + x - 12$, on which interval(s) is $f$ increasing?
  1. $(-\infty,-4)$ and $(3,\infty)$
  2. $(-4,\infty)$
  3. $(-4,3)$
  4. $(-\infty,3)$

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: A. $(-\infty,-4)$ and $(3,\infty)$
Solution / scoring notes: A function increases where its first derivative is positive.
U5-SUM-V2-Q06
Section 5.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 2

Use the first derivative test for $f'(x)=x^{2} - 1$ to classify the critical numbers.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: Local maximum at $x=-1$; local minimum at $x=1$.
Solution / scoring notes: Make a sign chart for the factored derivative and classify each sign change.
U5-SUM-V2-Q07
Section 5.2 | Summative Assessment | Unit-Synthesis | DOK 2

Unit 5 Summative - Version 2

The figure is the graph of $f'$. Which method directly determines intervals where $f$ increases?
  1. Compare endpoint values of $f$
  2. Read where $f'$ is above the x-axis
  3. Find where $f''$ is positive
  4. Read where $f$ is above the x-axis

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: B. Read where $f'$ is above the x-axis
Solution / scoring notes: Monotonicity of the original function is controlled by the sign of its first derivative.

Unit 5 Summative - Version 2

Two students classify a critical number. One uses only $f'(c)=0$; the other shows $f'$ changes from positive to negative at $c$. Compare the evidence and state which conclusion is justified.

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Keep the final response to one clear mathematical statement. Focus the response on the extrema evidence strategy requested here.

Answer: The sign-change argument justifies a local maximum; derivative zero alone does not classify the point.
Solution / scoring notes: The first derivative test requires behavior on both sides of the critical number.

Unit 5 Summative - Version 2

For $f''(x)=x^{2} + x - 2$, where is $f$ concave up?
  1. $(-2,1)$
  2. $(-2,\infty)$
  3. $(-\infty,-2)$ and $(1,\infty)$
  4. $(-\infty,1)$

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: C. $(-\infty,-2)$ and $(1,\infty)$
Solution / scoring notes: Concave up corresponds to a positive second derivative.
U5-SUM-V2-Q10

Unit 5 Summative - Version 2

If $f'(-3)=0$ and $f''(-3)=-5$, what does the second derivative test conclude?
  1. local minimum
  2. no classification is possible
  3. inflection point
  4. local maximum

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: D. local maximum
Solution / scoring notes: At a critical point, the sign of the second derivative classifies local curvature when it is nonzero.

Unit 5 Summative - Version 2

Which relationship is always correct on an interval where $f''(x)\gt 0$?
  1. $f'$ is increasing
  2. $f'$ is positive
  3. $f$ is increasing
  4. $f$ is positive

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: A. $f'$ is increasing
Solution / scoring notes: Positive second derivative means the first derivative is increasing.

  1. vertical tangent
  2. local minimum
  3. inflection point only
  4. local maximum

Use AP-calculus notation and include the requested justification or exact value.

Answer: B. local minimum
Solution / scoring notes: A negative-to-positive first derivative sign change means decreasing then increasing.

A rectangle has perimeter 40. Which one-variable objective correctly represents its area if one side is $x$?
  1. $A(x)=x^2+20$
  2. $A(x)=x(40-x)$
  3. $A(x)=x(20-x)$
  4. $A(x)=2x+2(20-x)$

Answer: C. $A(x)=x(20-x)$
Solution / scoring notes: Use the perimeter constraint to express the other side, then multiply side lengths.

  1. $0$
  2. $5$

  4. $10$

Answer: D. $10$

Answer: $x=5$ cm
